a:T833,方法的关键是把“部分 mask 的离散序列”嵌入连续空间，再在这个空间上定义从部分 mask 状态到干净序列的随机插值与速度场学习。具体地，MLFM 使用 Brownian bridge 作为 stochastic interpolant，连接 embedded partially masked sequence 与 embedded clean sequence。这样做有两个好处。其一，连续状态在所有位置上联合演化，不需要像传统 MDM 那样在反向过程里对 masked positions 做条件独立分解，因此 few-step 时能更自然地表达位置间依赖。其二，在 Brownian bridge 的边界，插值退化回嵌入后的 partially masked sequence，这与预训练 MDM 的输入形式对齐，于是可以直接用已有 MDM 权重做 warm start，而不是从头训练一个 flow LM。训练上，模型学习的是条件速度场，目标与 flow matching / stochastic interpolant 系列方法一致，但 conditioning 结构保留了 any-position generation：未 mask 的 token 作为已知上下文，mask 的位置由连续流逐步推向干净 token embedding。推断上，论文提出两项关键机制。第一是新的 classifier-free guidance 形式。与常见 CFG 直接在条件/无条件预测间线性外推不同，这里为了隔离 clean tokens 对速度场的贡献，会在 reference velocity 中对 clean tokens 再加噪，从而更稳定地估计“条件信息带来的修正量”。第二是 online token promotion：如果某个位置的 posterior mode 概率达到 1-ε，就立即把该位置提交为 clean token，并用其干净 embedding 替换连续状态。这样一来，已解决 token 不必等到最后一步才固定，而是能在后续步骤中立刻作为上下文参与其它位置的生成。这个设计与 MDM 的逐步 unmasking 有相似之处，但不同点在于：MDM 的反向转移通常仍在离散空间逐步采样，MLFM 则是在连续耦合动力学中做“提前离散化”的局部提交。论文真正新颖的地方不只是把 mask 加进 flow，而是把 masking 变成 flow 过程中的结构性边界条件和采样控制机制。b:T7c7,The core idea is to embed partially masked discrete sequences into a continuous space and learn a conditional flow from those partially observed states to clean token sequences. MLFM uses a Brownian bridge as the stochastic interpolant between an embedded partially masked sequence and the embedded clean sequence. This choice is important for two reasons. First, the latent state evolves jointly across positions, so the model is not forced into the position-wise independence approximation used by standard masked diffusion models. That makes few-step generation better aligned with the true dependency structure of language. Second, at the boundary of the Brownian bridge, the interpolant reduces to the embedded partially masked sequence, which matches the input structure of pretrained MDMs. This enables warm-start adaptation from a large masked diffusion model instead of training a flow LM from scratch. Training follows the stochastic-interpolant / flow-matching view: the network learns a conditional velocity field that transports the continuous latent state toward clean token embeddings while conditioning on observed, unmasked tokens. Inference adds two mechanisms. The first is a new form of classifier-free guidance. Rather than only contrasting conditional and unconditional predictions, the method noises clean tokens inside the reference velocity so that the contribution of clean context can be isolated more cleanly. The second is online token promotion: once the posterior mode at a position exceeds a confidence threshold of 1-ε, that token is immediately committed and its clean embedding is inserted into the state. This means resolved tokens become usable context for later denoising steps instead of remaining noisy until the end. That is the main behavioral difference from standard FLMs, which keep decoding all positions throughout generation, and from standard MDMs, which iteratively unmask in discrete space but still rely on factorized reverse transitions.c:T4ce,The experiments adapt a pretrained 1028M-parameter MDM from Nie et al. (2025a) into an MLFM and evaluate it on GSM8K and MT-Bench. The paper positions this as the first scaling of flow-based language models to instruction-following and reasoning-style downstream generation. Empirically, MLFM improves over SMDM and similarly sized autoregressive baselines on MT-Bench, and shows encouraging, though still early-stage, performance on GSM8K. The most informative results are the sampling ablations rather than any single benchmark number: the proposed guidance method and token-promotion mechanism both contribute meaningful gains, suggesting that the bottleneck in few-step non-AR generation is not only the training objective but also whether high-confidence tokens can be turned into useful context early enough. The paper also argues that warm-starting from a pretrained MDM materially reduces training cost; learning stable continuous dynamics for language from scratch would be substantially more expensive. The abstract does not expose the full numeric table, so the main results and appendix ablations should be read together, especially the comparisons across sampling step counts, promotion on/off, and guidance strength.d:T4cd,Read it in four passes. First, use the introduction and background to pin down the two limitations being combined: factorized reverse transitions in masked diffusion models and all-position decoding in standard flow language models. Second, study the definition of the Brownian-bridge stochastic interpolant; this is the conceptual center of the paper because it preserves the partially masked boundary condition while enabling coupled continuous evolution. Third, read the warm-start training section carefully to understand how pretrained MDMs are reused and what exactly is inherited. Fourth, spend time on the sampling algorithm, especially the modified classifier-free guidance and the token-promotion rule, because these determine whether the model actually behaves like an iterative reasoning generator rather than a generic continuous denoiser. On the experimental side, prioritize the main results, the few-step setting, the sampling ablations, and any qualitative examples. The three questions worth keeping in mind are: why masked diffusion breaks in the few-step regime, why flow models benefit from explicit masking structure for reasoning tasks, and why early promotion changes the downstream generation trajectory.e:T421,The model contains a D-layer Transformer block unit plus a dedicated correction FFN. During sequential generation, the current embedding e_t is combined with the cached block output from the previous position, z_{t-1}. The correction FFN produces a modified input for the block, so the token enters the block with information from the past already injected. Two design choices make the scheme trainable. First, the correction is non-cumulative: x^(k)=e+F_theta(x^(k-1), e), rather than a residual sum of K separate corrections. Iterations are used to approach a fixed point, not to accumulate depth. Second, the correction is past-only: position t depends on z_{t-1} and e_t, so at inference time the needed state is already cached. For training, the paper avoids T-step BPTT over the sequence. It unrolls the correction process K times over all positions in parallel, with K fixed and much smaller than the sequence length. The authors also describe a conversion path for pretrained Transformers by adding a zero-initialized correction FFN and fine-tuning.f:T409,The experiments cover OpenWebText, Wikipedia, and a synthetic pointer-chasing task, with widths from C=50 to 2048, depths from D=1 to 23, and block sizes of 64, 256, 512, and 1024. The reported headline comparisons are depth-efficiency results. A D=5, C=1120 context-ready model outperforms a 12-layer, C=768 Transformer and generates about 1.7x faster on an A100. With K=10, a D=1, C=2048 context-ready model outperforms a 6-layer, C=1088 Transformer and gives about a 2.6x inference speedup. Sequential inference matches K=10 training within 0.01 perplexity, suggesting that the fixed-point approximation used in training transfers cleanly to streaming inference in the tested regimes. On pointer chasing, a D=1 variant trained with BPTT solves all 10 composition levels, while standard Transformers show a depth-dependent staircase pattern. Ablations indicate that K=5 is usually enough for convergence when D>=5, that the architecture benefits from width and longer contexts, and that a dedicated correction network is necessary.10:T5d4,The method has two stages. Offline, the authors build a task-specific bank of draft trees. Starting from a small tree, they greedily expand frontier nodes and score each one-node augmentation via self-rollout, estimating mean accepted length. A subtle point is that candidate trees are first scored with a larger joint tree in a one-step what-if probe, which makes the estimate mildly optimistic; they therefore recalibrate candidates using each tree’s own proposal, attention mask, and KV-cache update before final ranking. They then evaluate the full acceptance frontier in the actual end-to-end decoding loop, including prefill, tree decoding, posterior evaluation, verification, and KV updates, and keep only throughput-Pareto-optimal trees. Online, these trees are stored in a precompiled TopologyBank containing masks, position offsets, and gather indices, so switching trees is an O(1) pointer swap rather than a kernel rebuild. The scheduler reads readily available signals such as base-model top-1 probability, EAGLE-2 path value, and recent acceptance history, and selects a tree per step. Three policies are studied: a threshold ladder, a static task-optimal rule, and a hysteretic binary policy with separate on/off thresholds. The hysteretic policy works best because it captures the main large-tree/small-tree trade-off while avoiding per-step oscillation. The core idea is simple: the optimal speculation depth should track local predictability, not benchmark-level averages.11:T443,Experiments focus on the low-latency batch_size=1 regime and cover GSM8K, HumanEval, and ShareGPT. EntMTP-τ outperforms Hydra, Medusa, and the static EntMTP* rule on all three tasks. On GSM8K it reaches 112.0 tok/s versus 102.4 tok/s for Hydra, a 9.4% gain and 3.13× speedup over autoregressive decoding. On HumanEval it reaches 124.7 tok/s, a 14.0% gain over Hydra and 3.26× over AR. On ShareGPT it reaches 117.5 tok/s versus 109.0 tok/s, a 7.8% gain and 3.47× over AR. Response perplexity stays within 0.022 of Hydra across settings, indicating that the gains come from better scheduling rather than a change in the verification rule. The most informative analysis is the task dependence of acceptance signals: on ShareGPT, recent acceptance history predicts next-step accept length with EMA correlation 0.49, whereas on GSM8K and HumanEval the strongest entropy-based predictors are at least 2× weaker, with |r|≤0.22. The paper also shows that maximizing accepted depth is not the same as maximizing throughput, because larger trees increase verifier and posterior-evaluation cost.12:T46e,The method scores each layer by how much full attention at that layer improves answer-token likelihood under a SWAA-style setup.
- Setup: During prefill, each layer can be Full Attention (FA) or Sliding-Window Attention (SWA); during decoding, all layers use FA (full-attention decode) to isolate prefill degradation.
- Per-layer scoring: For a prompt+answer pair, compute mean NLL on answer tokens under (i) all layers SWA in prefill, and (ii) only layer ℓ switched to FA while all others remain SWA. The score Δℓ is the NLL reduction from (ii) vs (i). Larger Δℓ implies that layer benefits more from global context during prefill.
- Selection: Average Δℓ over a small long-context calibration set, then pick the top-k layers (greedy) for the FA budget.
- Deconfounding check: Repeat ranking with short prompts and show low rank correlation with long-prompt ranking (Spearman ρ=0.306), arguing the signal is specific to long-range needs rather than generic layer sensitivity.
Trade-off: requires L+1 forward passes per calibration example (no gradients, no generation), but it is a one-off cost amortized over deployment.13:T47f,VASAE (Vocabulary-Aligned SAE) adds a soft anchoring objective to standard SAEs so that each learned decoder direction stays close to some token embedding.
- Training target: SAEs are trained on post-residual stream activations in decoder-only Transformers; dictionary features are decoder columns; sparse codes reconstruct activations.
- Key issue: standard SAE features can reconstruct well while being far from vocabulary directions; conversely, hard-tying the SAE decoder to the token embedding matrix overly constrains the dictionary and hurts reconstruction.
- Proposed fix: augment the SAE loss with a vocabulary-aligned anchor term. For each feature direction, identify its nearest token embedding as an anchor and penalize distance to it with coefficient λ_anchor (soft constraint). After training, the nearest token string becomes the feature’s intrinsic name.
- Diagnostics: define a nearest-token alignment score and report the fraction of features exceeding a threshold (0.8 in the paper).
Design choice is explicit: trade some dictionary freedom for nameability, while keeping reconstruction competitive via soft (not hard) alignment.3:["$","$L9",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-06-30","generated_at":"2026-06-30T00:50:59.545297+00:00","stats":{"total":83,"starred":1,"must_read":5,"skim":38,"glance":40,"by_category":{"llm_pretrain":3,"scaling":2,"architecture":9,"efficient":7,"eval":15,"multimodal":1,"multimodal_gen":9,"post_train":14,"mechanism":11,"coding_agent":1,"agent":11},"trending_repos":6,"trending_hf":10,"fetch_status":{"arxiv":"ok","huggingface":"failed","blog":"ok","twitter":"empty","github_trending":"ok","hf_trending":"ok"},"fetch_errors":{"huggingface":"exhausted 5 retries; last error: Client error '400 Bad Request' for url 'https://huggingface.co/api/daily_papers?date=2026-06-30'\nFor more information check: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Status/400"},"last_fetch_attempt":1782782215.076859},"categories":[{"id":"llm_pretrain","emoji":"🧠","name":"LLM 预训练","description":"文本LLM预训练的训练侧：优化器、学习率日程、训练 recipe、训练稳定性、并行/分布式训练、重磅技术报告（数据为核心的工作归 pretraining_data；Scaling Law 归 scaling；不含 diffusion/flow/any-order 等建模范式）","papers":[{"id":"2606.27715","title":"Aurora: A Leverage-Aware Spectral Optimizer","abstract":"We show that for tall matrix parameters, like projection matrices in the MLP layers, the Muon update can have row norms that are arbitrarily non-uniform. Masked diffusion models support any-position parallel generation, but in the few-step regime they usually rely on a reverse process that factorizes across masked positions, and that approximation becomes weakest exactly where parallel decoding should be most useful. Flow language models avoid this by moving generation into a continuous space where token positions evolve jointly, but standard FLMs are less natural for iterative language generation in which some tokens should become fixed early while others remain unresolved. The paper asks whether one can combine the any-position conditional structure of masked models with the coupled dynamics of flow models, so that generation remains parallel yet can still proceed through partially resolved intermediate states.","importance":"This is a must-read for pretraining researchers because it pushes on an alternative language-modeling paradigm rather than making a local improvement inside the autoregressive setup. The contribution matters at three levels. First, it introduces Masked Language Flow Models, a hybrid that fills the gap between masked diffusion models with poor few-step behavior and flow language models without explicit masking structure. Second, it provides a practical training path by warm-starting from pretrained MDMs, which is important if this family is ever to scale beyond small proof-of-concept experiments. Third, it proposes an inference mechanism with classifier-free guidance and early token promotion, giving the model a more reasoning-like iterative generation dynamic. If this line develops further, it could affect not only sampling efficiency but also how intermediate states are represented in language modeling and how non-AR pretraining objectives are designed for reasoning-heavy tasks.","technical_approach":"$b","experiments":"$c","reading_guide":"$d","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇会直接影响你对“flow LM 是否注定不适合复杂文本生成”的 prior；先看它如何把 masking 嵌进 continuous flow，再检查 few-step 结果是否真比 MDM 干净，值得尽快复现小规模版。","en":"This one can change your prior on whether flow LMs are inherently awkward for complex text generation; first inspect how masking is inserted into the continuous flow, then verify whether the few-step results are actually cleaner than MDMs—worth a small-scale reproduction."},"metadata":{"institutions":["University of Oxford"],"authors":["Iskander Azangulov","Kianoosh Ashouritaklimi","Leo Zhang","Simon Vary","Patrick Rebeschini"],"notable_authors":["Patrick Rebeschini"],"tags":["diffusion-lm","flow-language-model","masked-generation","non-autoregressive","few-step-sampling"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27617","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27617","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2606.27567","title":"On the Inseparability of Instructions and Data in Shared-Embedding Sequence Models","abstract":"Prompt injection is the top security risk for LLM-integrated applications, yet every defense proposed so far has been broken. 中，可以零额外迭代地计算当前修正。训练时不能按完整序列做 T 步 BPTT，所以作者在全序列上并行 unroll K 次，K 是小常数，实验中 K=5 或 K=10。这样计算图深度随 K 增长，而不是随序列长度 T 增长。已有 Transformer 也可以通过加入 zero-initialized correction FFN 再微调的方式转换。","experiments":"实验覆盖 compute 都有启发，因为“接受深度”不是静态模型属性，而是 workload-conditioned 的动态量。贡献量级属于工程上扎实有效：不训练新模型，只加一个调度层，就能在 batch size 1 下稳定超过固定树基线，同时几乎不改变输出分布质量。","technical_approach":"方法分两段。第一段是离线构建 task-specific 的 tree bank。作者沿用 Hydra 类方法的 greedy tree expansion 思路，从小树开始逐步加节点；每次在 frontier 上评估所有单节点扩展，用 self-rollout 估计 mean accept length。这里有一个细节：候选树 T∪{ν} 的打分是在更大的 joint tree 上做 one-step what-if probe，因此估计略偏乐观，作者随后再用 candidate 自己的 proposal/attention mask/KV update 做一次 recalibration。之后不是只选 accept depth 最大的树，而是把整条 acceptance frontier 放到真实端到端 decode loop 里测 wall-clock tok/s，连 prefill 和 verifier 开销一起算，得到 throughput-Pareto frontier，只保留真正不被支配的树。第二段是在线调度。作者把这些树的 attention masks、position offsets、gather indices 预编译进 TopologyBank，因此切换树只需 O(1) 指针替换，不需要重建 kernel。在线策略读取现成特征，如 base model top-1 probability、EAGLE-2 path value、近期接受历史等，然后在每一步选择树。文中给了三种策略：EntMTP-l 是多阈值阶梯规则；EntMTP* 是针对单任务离线选出的静态最优树；EntMTP-τ 是带 hysteresis 的二值开关，用不同 on/off threshold 避免逐步抖动。真正效果最好的是后者，因为它在大树/小树之间切换足够简单，且能抑制高频 oscillation。论文的关键判断是：树大小的最优点由“当前 token 区域的可预测性”决定，而不是由整个 benchmark 的平均统计决定。","experiments":"实验集中在 batch_size=1 的低延迟场景，任务覆盖 GSM8K、HumanEval、ShareGPT 三类分布。结果上，EntMTP-τ 全面优于 Hydra、Medusa 和静态 comput","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-26T07:20:23Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2606_27791.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"长上下文推理里，用“部分层全注意力 成本，但“到底该保留哪些层的全注意力”缺少可靠、与下游准确率直接相关的选择准则；固定周期选层和基于注意力权重的启发式在不同模型/任务上不稳定，且同样预算下选层差异会带来巨大精度落差。","importance":"这是一个偏部署侧的推理适配问题，不直接改变预训练，但对“预训练为全注意力、部署改稀疏注意力”的落地链路很关键：给出了一个与任务目标一致（答案 token 的 设定：prefill 阶段每层可选 Full Attention(FA) 或 Sliding-Window Attention(SWA)；decode 阶段保持全层 FA（full-attention decode），以尽量隔离 prefill 稀疏化带来的信息丢失。\n2) 对每一层 ℓ 计算一个增益分数 Δℓ：在“其他层都用 SWA”的基线下，仅把第 ℓ 层切到 FA，比较答案 token 的平均 NLL 下降量（NLL(all-SWA) − NLL(only-ℓ-FA)）。Δℓ 越大，表示该层的全注意力越能改善从长 prompt 到答案的可预测性。\n3) 在一个小的长上下文校准集上对 Δℓ 做平均，然后直接取 top-k 层作为给定 FA 预算下的保留层集合 S（贪心选择）。\n4) 论文还做了“去混淆”校准：用短 prompt 也算一遍层排序，与长 prompt 的排序相关性较低（Spearman ρ=0.306），用来支持该信号确实在捕捉长程依赖需求，而不是泛化的‘某层更敏感’。\n取舍上，这个方法把问题从‘看注意力图/结构先验’转成‘直接测量对答案概率的贡献’，代价是需要 L+1 次前向（每个样本），但不需要训练、无梯度、一次性校准可摊销。","experiments":"主要实验在 Qwen3-4B-Thinking-2507（36 层）+ LongMemEval（500 样本，6 类长程记忆任务，平均 prompt ~24k token）。推理配置沿用 SWAA：window=2048、keep-first-10 attention sinks、decode 全 FA。\n关键结果：\n- 在仅保留 1/4 层 FA 的预算下，NLL-guided 选层在 LongMemEval 达到 64.6% accuracy；与 1/2-FA 的周期性选层基线 65.0% 接近，但 FA 预算减半。\n- 相比 SWAA 报告的周期性 1/4-FA 基线，提升 10.4 个百分点（说明“选哪些层”比“选多少层”更关键）。\n- 选层好坏差异很大：同为 1/4-FA，在 Qwen3-4B 上好/坏选择可差 26+ 个百分点（强调该问题的敏感性）。\n- 校准成本：用 64 个长样本（16k–32k）做校准；每样本 L+1 次前向；4 GPU 约 15 分钟。以 24k prompt 估算，约 1,354 次请求后摊销回本。","reading_guide":"泛读建议（约 20–30 分钟）：\n- 先看 Method 里 Δℓ 的定义与“只在 prefill 对 prompt 用 SWA、答案 token 仍全上下文”的实现细节；这是方法成立的关键假设。\n- 重点看实验部分关于“1/4-FA 逼近 1/2-FA”的主结果，以及“好/坏选层差 26pp”的敏感性描述，理解为什么需要可测量的选层准则。\n- 看 long vs short prompt 的相关性分析（Spearman ρ=0.306），这是论文用来证明信号指向‘长程注意力需求’而非一般层脆弱性的主要证据。\n- 如果要复现/落地，优先翻 Appendix 的实现与成本估算：L+1 前向如何做、校准集如何选、以及 break-even 的计算口径。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Hybrid attention for long-context inference (keeping full attention in only a subset of layers while using sliding-window attention elsewhere during prefill) can cut quadratic cost, but the key decision—which layers should retain full attention under a fixed budget—has no robust, accuracy-aligned criterion. Periodic patterns and attention-weight heuristics are brittle, and layer choice can swing downstream accuracy dramatically.","importance":"This is primarily an inference-time adaptation paper rather than a pretraining recipe change, but it matters for the common mismatch where models are pretrained with dense attention and deployed with sparse/limited attention. a specific failure mode in long-horizon LLM agents: after a fact has been updated, can the agent maintain the current value and stop relying on the superseded one when it only has a bounded, self-written memory?","importance":"The contribution is more relevant to post-training and agent memory training than to base pretraining itself. Its useful point is the decomposition of long-term memory into an update-maintenance problem, rather than a generic retrieval or compression problem. The experiments suggest that larger context access and larger memory budgets do not automatically teach the policy how to overwrite stale facts. That is a concrete signal-design lesson for training memory-using models.","technical_approach":"The paper introduces Supersede, a trainable environment for conversational fact updates. An agent reads sessions one at a time and maintains a bounded notes field. The raw sessions are not fed again at answer time, so the final answer depends only on the agent-maintained memory. The main reward is r_cur, a binary check that the final answer conveys the current value. When superseded values are available, the environment can add r_stale, a penalty for asserting an obsolete value, although the reported training runs optimize r_cur alone. Scoring is programmatic, using normalized variant matching with a token-overlap fallback, so the setup avoids an auxiliary LLM judge. The training experiment fine-tunes Qwen2.5-3B with GRPO and LoRA. Compared with prior long-memory evaluations, the key change is that supersession correctness becomes an optimization target, not only an evaluation metric.","experiments":"The main diagnostic result is a gap between full-context answering and bounded self-maintained memory. On the knowledge-update subset of LongMemEval, the authors report that gpt-5.4 reaches about 92% with full context but drops to 77% with bounded memory; the paired McNemar test gives p=0.0033. The gap persists across model scale while full-context accuracy saturates. Scaling the conversation length makes the problem worse: with a 24x increase in conversation size, accuracy falls from 68% to 28%. Increasing the memory budget proportionally does not produce a detectable recovery in the n=25 setting, which argues against a simple compression-ratio explanation. Training helps but does not solve the task. GRPO fine-tuning of Qwen2.5-3B improves held-out real-conversation supersession accuracy from 9.0% to 16.7%. The paper also notes that synthetic templated updates are saturated by frontier models under full context, reaching 100%, so they are not sufficient for diagnosing the real failure.","reading_guide":"A 20–30 minute skim is enough for most pretraining researchers. Focus on the definition of the supersession gap, the no-re-feed environment constraint, and the three empirical sections: full-context versus bounded memory, conversation scaling versus memory scaling, and GRPO training. The procedural reward is worth checking because it shows how a forgetting-aware metric can be converted into a training signal. The synthetic-task details can be read lightly. Without reducing reconstruction quality compar","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-26T10:30:56Z","category_id":"mechanism","cover_url":"/covers/2606_27941.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"残差流 的几何关系不对齐，导致解释时只能靠事后找激活上下文或另跑自动解释器，命名不稳定、成本高，也难以把特征与模型的词表接口（embedding/readout）直接关联。","importance":"这是机制解释/可解释性方向的工作，不直接改进预训练指标，但它把 SAE 字典与词表几何对齐，提供一种更可复现的特征标识方式（nearest-token intrinsic name）。对预训练研究的间接价值在于：\n- 更容易把“某层学到的方向”与 token 级语义接口联系起来，降低分析门槛；\n- 为后续做数据/训练动态的表征追踪提供更稳定的坐标系（至少在浅层/中层）。\n不过它并不保证 token 名就是语义真名，更多是一个几何锚点；深层不稳定也提示了词表几何未必覆盖高层抽象特征。","technical_approach":"VASAE（Vocabulary-Aligned SAE）的核心是给 SAE 增加一个“软锚定”目标，让每个字典特征方向靠近某个 token embedding，从而训练后可以用最近邻 token 作为该特征的内生名字。\n- 基础对象：在 decoder-only Transformer 的 post-residual stream 上训练 SAE；字典是 SAE decoder 权重矩阵的列向量（feature directions），稀疏码是每个特征的系数。\n- 问题根源：标准 SAE 只优化重构+稀疏，学到的方向可能远离任何 token embedding；而把 decoder 硬绑定为 token embedding 矩阵又会损失重构能力。\n- 方法：在标准 SAE 损失之外加入 anchor loss。对每个 feature direction，找到其最近的 token embedding 作为 anchor，并用一个系数 λ_anchor 鼓励该 feature 靠近该 embedding（“软”而非硬绑定，保留字典自由度）。训练完成后，直接用最近 token 的字符串作为 intrinsic token name。\n- 诊断指标：定义 nearest-token alignment score，并用阈值（文中用 0.8 cutoff）统计有多少特征能被可靠地对齐到词表方向。\n方法取舍很明确：牺牲一部分字典几何自由度，换取可命名性；并通过对比“硬绑定 decoder”来说明必须是软约束而不是直接把字典等同词表。","experiments":"实验覆盖 GPT-2-small 与 Llama-3.1-8B 的 post-residual stream SAE。\n- 重构质量：VASAE 在测试设置下与标准 SAE 的重构指标接近。文中给出 GPT-2-small 的 variance explained (VE) 约 0.965，Llama-3.1-8B 约 0.931；而“decoder 硬绑定 token embedding”的方案重构更差。\n- 对齐覆盖率：在 GPT-2-small 的浅层到中浅层（L0–L10），用 alignment cutoff=0.8 时约 89%–94% 的特征可对齐到某个 token embedding。\n- 在 Llama-3.1-8B 上，较大的锚定系数 λ_anchor=5e-3 时，L0 可达 92.8% 对齐，但在代表性的最后层仍不稳定（提示深层特征与词表几何更脱钩，或锚定会与重构/稀疏目标冲突）。\n- 论文还给了 case studies：展示在具体上下文中被激活的特征，其 intrinsic token name 作为标识符如何使用（更像“坐标标签”而非严格语义解释）。","reading_guide":"泛读建议（约 30–45 分钟）：\n- 先看 Introduction 里对“字典几何 vs 词表几何脱钩”的动机，以及为什么 hard-tying 不够。\n- 重点看方法部分 anchor objective 的定义、最近邻 token 的选取方式、以及 alignment score 的诊断口径；这些决定了‘命名’到底在多大程度上可信。\n- 实验部分优先看两类对比：VASAE vs 标准 SAE（重构不掉多少）以及 VASAE vs hard-tied（说明软约束必要）。\n- 如果要用于分析预训练动态，建议特别关注跨层的对齐率曲线：浅层高对齐、深层不稳定这一现象本身就值得解释（词表接口在不同深度的作用边界）。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Residual-stream SAEs decompose Transformer activations into sparse features, but those features typically lack intrinsic, vocabulary-grounded names. Because SAE dictionary directions are learned purely for reconstruction+sparsity, they can be geometrically far from any token embedding, forcing post-hoc naming via context inspection or separate interpretation pipelines.","importance":"This is interpretability infrastructure rather than a pretraining improvement, but it tightens the connection between learned features and the model’s vocabulary interface by aligning SAE directions to token embeddings.
